maṟunāṭ kāli 6 maṇikkut taṉ makaṉ kaṇṇaṉi maturikku aṉuppiviṭṭu, maṉiviyiyum aruvikku aṉuppiviṭṭu, tāṉ kuḷippataṟkāka eṇṇeyiyum tuṇṭiyum eṭuttuvara vēliyāḷiyum aṉuppiviṭṭu, aruviyi nōkkit tiruvācakattip pāṭiyapaṭi naṭantu koṇṭiruntār. aimpataṭi tūram naṭantatum kiyi ūṉṟi uṭkārntār. tali cāyntatu. uṭaṉē ellōrum cērntu avarkaḷi oru tuppaṭṭiyil kiṭattit tūkki vantu paṭukkiyil kiṭatti maruttuvarukkum ceyti aṉuppi, maruttuvarum uṭaṉē vantucērntār. ivi aṉittum ceytu muṭiya ēḻu nimiṭaṅkaḷē piṭittaṉa. maruttuvar avarip paricōtittu uyir pirintu ēḻu nimiṭaṅkaḷ āyiṉa. eṉak kūṟiviṭṭār. ellōrum kataṟi aḻutōm. avaratu poṉṉuṭalukku mutal māliyiṭum nili eṉakku nēriṭṭatu. pala iṭaṅkaḷukku tolipēcic ceytikaḷ paṟantaṉa. makaṉ kaṇṇaṉiyum irājapāḷiyattiṟkuc ceyti aṉuppi vaḻimaṟittut tiruppi aḻittuk koṇṭōm. kārkaḷ palavantaṉa. avaratu poṉṉuṭaluṭaṉ nāṉum, rātā ammiyāruṭaṉ eṉ maṉiviyumāka aḻutu koṇṭē maturi vantu cērntōm. mīṉāṭci āliyiṉ uḷḷum puṟamum, avaratu illattiṉ uḷḷēyum veḷiyilum, vītiyiṉ vaḻineṭukilum makkaḷ kūṭṭam kaṭal alikaḷ pōṉṟu peruki niṉṟu kataṟi aḻutu koṇṭirunta kāṭci eṅkaḷ tuṉpatti mēlum vaḷaracceytatu. poṉṉuṭalik kāṇavanta makkaḷ kūṭṭam vikiyil kūṭum aḻakar tiruviḻāk kūṭṭattiyum miñciyiruntatu.

niṉivuc ciṉṉam

avaratu uṭal erikkappaṭuvati nāṉ virumpavilli. avaratu māḷikiyiṉ piṉ puṟattilēyē aṭakkam ceytu, niṉivu ciṉṉam oṉṟu aṅku eḻuppa vēṇṭumeṉṟu eṇṇiṉēṉ. ataṟkākap peritum muyaṉṟēṉ. tōlviyiyē aṭintēṉ. iṟutiyil avaratu poṉmēṉi erikkappaṭṭup pōyiṟṟu.

eṉṟu kāṇpōm

karumuttuvi, kalittantiyi, toḻilatipari, peruñcelvari, koṭivaḷḷali, tamiḻaṟiñari, ēḻi paṅkāḷari, eḷiya vāḻviṉari, eḷiya uṭiyiṉari, iṉiya colliṉari, avaratu iṉmukatti, puṉcirippi, nām iṉi eṉṟum eṅkum kāṇappōvatilli. avaratu iḻappu tamiḻukku, tamiḻarkku, tamiḻakattiṟku pēriḻappāka muṭintatu. yār? yārukku āṟutal kūṟuvatu?

mella nakarntu cellum kālamtāṉ nam aṉivarkkum nallāṟutal kūṟavēṇṭum.

⁠vāḻka karumuttuviṉ pukaḻ!

﻿vaḷarka avar ceyta paṇikaḷ!





﻿﻿﻿toḻilatipar vi. cēṣacāyi toḻilaṟiñar



tiruccirāppaḷḷi pala tamiḻaṟiñarkaṉiyum, icip pulavarkaḷiyum, uyarnta naṭikarkaḷiyum, ciṟanta vaṇikarkaḷiyum peṟṟu utaviyatu maṭṭumalla, atu nalla toḻilaṟiñarkaḷiyum peṟṟuttantu perumiyaṭintirukkiṟatu. attakiya taliciṟanta toḻilaṟiñarkaḷil tiru. vi. cēṣacāyi avarkaḷum oruvar.

perumakaṉ

tamiḻakattiṉ perunakaraṅkaḷil oṉṟākiya tirucciyiṉ kaṭum iruḷippōkka miṉ viḷakki ēṟṟivittu oḷitanta perumakaṉ. immuyaṟciyil kolli maliyiliruntu miṉcāram koṇṭuvara mēlināṭṭu aṟiñarkaḷ muyaṉṟu tōlvi aṭintatik kaṇṭum, perum muyaṟci ceytu veṟṟikaṇṭa pēraṟiñar avar.

āli atipar

tirucci māvaṭṭam maṭṭumallāmal, piṟa māvaṭṭaṅkaḷ palavaṟṟilum viññāṉat tuṟiyil ālikaḷi amittu palvēṟu poruḷkaḷi uṇṭu paṇṇik koṭuttu innāṭṭiṉ tēvikaḷi nirappivanta āli atipar avar. ēḻi paṅkāḷar

tamatu nuṇṇaṟivāl, aruntiṟaṉāl, ēṟakkuṟiya 7000 pērkaḷukku uḻikka vaḻivakuttu avarkaḷiṉ uḻippiṉ mūlam ēṟakkuṟiya irupatāyiram pērkaḷ paciyiṉṟi vāḻa vakiceytu vāḻntu maṟinta ēḻi paṅkāḷar avar.

tamiḻp pulavar

mikak kuṟinta nāṭkaḷil eṉṉiṭam, tirukkuṟaḷip paṭittu ārāyat toṭaṅki, mika virivil kuṟaḷ muḻuvataṟkum poruḷ kūṟum aṟiviyum āṟṟaliyum peṟṟuviṭṭa oru ciṟanta tamiḻppulavar avar.

cāstirikaḷ

vālmīki irāmāyaṇatti eḻutteṇṇip paṭittatōṭu, kamparāmāyaṇattiyum naṉku kaṟṟu, iraṇṭiṟkum uḷḷa oṟṟumi vēṟṟumikaḷiyum kaṟpaṉi nayaṅkaḷiyum eṭuttuk kūṟumpoḻutu oru ciṟanta cāstiriyākak kāṭciyaḷittavar avar.

eḷiya tōṟṟam

avaratu āṭamparamaṟṟa eḷiya tōṟṟamum, aṉpukalanta iṉcollum naṇpari maṭṭumalla, pakivariyum paṇiyac ceyyum valimi vāyntavi.

uyarnta paṇpu

piṟaruṭiya tēviyi uṇaramaṟuppatum, piṟaruṭiya urimiyi oppamaṟuppatum ākiya iraṇṭum maṭṭumē nāṭṭil naṭipeṟum pōrāṭṭaṅkaḷukkuk kāraṇam eṉpati naṉkuṇarntu, ataṟkēṟṟapaṭi naṭantu kāṭṭiya uyarnta paṇpāḷar avar. peruṅkuṇam

ēḻi makkaḷukku irakkam kāṭṭutal, māṟupaṭṭa karuttukaḷukku matippuk koṭuttal, piṭivātakkārarkaḷukku viṭṭuk koṭuttal, cakipput taṉmi, tārāḷa maṉappāṉmi ākiya aimperuṅ kuṇaṅkaḷiyum oruṅkē peṟṟirunta kuṇakkuṉṟu avar.

nallaṟiñar

kuṭumpattilum toḻililum paṅku peṟṟu, vāḻvilum tāḻvilum uṟṟa tuṇiyāki, eppōtum uṭaṉiruntu uḻittu maṟinta mittuṉar tiru. ār. cēṣacāyi avarkaḷi uḷḷattē vittu vāḻnta naṉṟi maṟavā nallaṟiñar avar.

cīrtiruttavāti

tāṉ tōṟṟiya teṉceṉṉi miṉvaḻaṅkum niṟuvaṉattiṟru eṉṉit talivaṉākki, ati naṭattum poṟuppi eṉṉiṭam oppuvittu makiḻntu, cāti vēṟṟumi, camaya vēṟṟumi, nāṭṭu vēṟṟumi, moḻi vēṟṟumi, kaṭci vēṟṟumi, niṟa vēṟṟumi, uṇavu vēṟṟumi ākiya aṉittiyum pārātu, ellōriyum tammavarāyk koṇṭu naṭantu kāṭṭiya ōr uyarnta cīrtiruttavāti avar.

pēccuk kaliñar

atikārikaḷōṭu pēcuvatu eppaṭi? aṉparkaḷōṭu pēcuvatu eppaṭi? nirvākikaḷōṭu pēcuvatu eppaṭi? toḻilāḷikaḷōṭu pēcuvatu eppaṭi? maṉiviyōṭu pēcuvatu eppaṭi? makkaḷōṭu pēcuvatu eppaṭi? eṉpati naṉkuṇarntu, arumiyākap pēcip piṟariyum tammip piṉpaṟṟip pēcumpaṭi ceyta pēccuk kaliñar avar. koṭi vaḷḷal

caliyāmal muyaṟcittu, ōyāmal uḻittu, nērmiyāka naṭantu, nallavaḻiyil poruḷit tēṭi, cikkaṉamākac celaviṭṭu, eñciya poruḷi nalla aṟa niliyaṅkaḷ palavaṟṟiṟku vāri vaḻaṅki makiḻnta koṭivaḷḷal avar.

pēriḻappu



ittakiya uyarnta toḻil nipuṇarum, ciṟanta moḻi aṟiñarum, niṟinta paṇpuṭiyavarum, kaṉinta colluṭiyavarum ākiya cāṉṟōr oruvari iḻantatu, potuvākat tamiḻakattiṟkum, kuṟippākat tirucci nakarukkum oru pēriḻappu ākum.

pukaḻuṭampu



avaratu poyyuṭal maṟintālum pukaḻuṭal maṟivataṟkilli. avar koṭi vaḻaṅkivanta pala aṟa niliyaṅkaḷum, toṭaṅkivinta pala toḻil niliyaṅkaḷum, ariyamaṅkalattil tōṟṟivitta toḻil nuṇukkap paḷḷiyum nilittu niṟkumvari, mēṭṭur kemikkals uḷḷavari, īrōṭu kākita āli uḷḷavari, avaratu pukaḻuṭampu aḻiyātu. eṉiṉum, avaratu peyarāl oru poṟiiyaṟ kallūriyiyum tōṟṟivikka vēṇṭiyatu tamiḻaka makkaḷiṉ nīṅkāk kaṭamikaḷil oṉṟākum.

āṟutal

“ippaṭippaṭṭa oruvar tirucciyil iruppati nāṉ ivvaḷavu nāḷ aṟiyāmaṟ pōṉēṉē” eṉṟu avar eṉṉippaṟṟi piṟariṭam kūṟiya coṟkaḷ eṉ kātil viḻuntapoḻututāṉ, “ippaṭippaṭṭa oruvar tirucciyil iruppati nāṉ ivvaḷavu nāḷ aṟiyāmaṟ pōṉēṉē” eṉṟu nāṉum eṇṇiṉēṉ. eṉatu kaṇkaḷ iṉpak kaṇṇīri tuḷirttaṉa. aṭuttu mika virivil nērnta avaratu pirivu tuṉpak kaṇṇīri utirkkumpaṭi ceytu viṭṭatu. nāṭu kalaṅkiṟṟu. nāmum kalaṅkiṉōm. yār yārukku āṟutal kūṟuvatu? mella nakarntu cellum kālamtāṉ nam aṉivarkkum nal āṟutal aḷikkavēṇṭum.





﻿ ﻿﻿﻿tamiḻ maruntukaḷ

ki. ā. pe. vicuvanātam



“āṅkila āṭci vantatum citta āyurvēta yūṉāṇi mutaliya nam nāṭṭup parampari vittiyaṅkaḷ aḻintoḻintaṉa. nam nāṭṭup pāṭṭiyiṉ vittiyamum pārttup pārttu ōṭiviṭṭatu. eṭuttataṟkellām ūci pōṭa ṭākṭarit tēṭum nilimiyum vanteytiyatu. ataṉāl, uṭampu maṭṭumiṉṟi uyirum pāḻākiyatu. ellām pāḻākiya piṉṉarē, tamiḻ makkaḷ taṅkaḷ paḻiya celvaṅkaḷit tēṭa muṟpaṭṭuḷḷārkaḷ. nāṭu viṭutalipeṟṟatum immuyaṟci mikavum ūkkattuṭaṉ naṭakkiṟatu. itaṟku utavi aḷikkum vakiyilum, paḻiya kimmuṟi vittiyaṅkaḷ aḻiyātu, pātukākkappaṭavēṇṭum eṉṉum avā mikutiyiṉālum aṟiñar vicuvanātam avarkaḷ tam vīṭṭuk kimmuṟikaḷil palavaṟṟi innūluḷ tantuḷḷār. ittakiya nūlkaḷ iṉṉum veḷivara vēṇṭum. mūla nōykku oṉpatu cikiccikaḷ kūṟappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. innūlil oruvar kiyil iruppatē oru vittiyar uṭaṉ iruppataṟku nikar eṉṟu collalām. aṟiñar vicuvanātam avarkaḷ iṉṉum pala kimmuṟikaḷi aritiṉ muyaṉṟu peṟṟu veḷiyiṭuvārēl, tamiḻ makkaḷukkup pērutavi ceytavarāvar!’

‘tiṉakaraṉ’ matippuri



15–11–53

pāri niliyam,

184, pirāṭvē, ceṉṉi–108.

﻿﻿﻿﻿﻿aintu celvaṅkaḷ

ki. ā. pe. vicuvanātam

The five different kinds of wealth, which the author discusses in this book are Mothor, Imagination, Old Age Health and Character. In the first essay he expatiaies the greatness of the mother who also expresses herself in the mother country and the mother tongue and drives home to the Reader on the necessity of paying his homage to everyone of them. In the second essay the learned author leads the student to imagine and find out for himself the several uses of the eye and the ear and by series of questions and answers arrives at the conclusion that everyone should see little, speak Hess, but hear more. In the next chapter entitled ‘Weakth of Old age’ the value of experience is mentioned and incidently, the virtue of truthfulness emphasised; it would have been better if the teaching intended to be brought forth by the chapter heading. is more boldly brought forth. The next chapter deals with the nesessity of Physical exercises and the last chapter is a story of two boys who after a long search for true Wealth come to the conclusion that only good character and good behaviour form true Wealth. The style of the book is simple and direct.

—The “HINDU”



19-4-'53





pāri niliyam

184, pirāṭvē, ceṉṉi-108

inta miṉṉūlip paṟṟi


uṅkaḷukku immiṉṉūl, iṇiya nūlakamāṉa, vikkimūlattil iruntu kiṭittuḷḷatu[1].

inta iṇiya nūlakam taṉṉārvalarkaḷāl vaḷarukiṟatu. vikkimūlam patiya taṉṉārvalarkaḷi varavēṟkiṟatu. tāṅkaḷum vikkimūlattil iṇintu mēlum pala miṉṉūlkaḷi aṉivarum paṭikkumāṟu ceyyalām.

mikunta akkaṟiyuṭaṉ meyppu ceytālum, miṉṉūlil piḻi ētēṉum iruntāl tayakkam illāmal, vikkimūlattil immiṉṉūliṉ pēccu pakkattil terivikkalām allatu piḻikaḷi nīṅkaḷē kūṭa cari ceyyalām.
